Understanding the PeopleTools-Only Upgrade Using the
PeopleSoft PeopleTools DPKs
 Use these steps to perform a PeopleTools-only upgrade to PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62.xx, using the PeopleSoft
 PeopleTools Deployment Packages (DPKs). The PeopleTools Client DPK is used first to deploy the PeopleTools
 Client, which includes the installation of Change Assistant. Subsequently, the PeopleTools DPKs are used to
 install mid-tier components and the PS_HOME for the new release.
 Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                              249
Performing a PeopleTools-Only Upgrade Using the PeopleSoft                                              Appendix B
PeopleTools DPKs
This diagram summarizes the scenario for performing a PeopleTools-only upgrade to PeopleSoft PeopleTools
8.62.xx using DPKs:
PeopleTools-only upgrade using DPKs.
This scenario assumes that:
•  You want to upgrade to PeopleTools 8.62.xx.
•  Your operating system is AIX, Linux, Microsoft Windows or Solaris.
Task B-1: Performing the PeopleTools-Only Upgrade Using DPKs
To use the DPKs to perform a PeopleTools-only upgrade:
250                                                                       Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix B                                                       Performing a PeopleTools-Only Upgrade Using the PeopleSoft
                                                                                           PeopleTools DPKs
1.  Stop and delete the domains for the existing (old) release.
    Stop and delete the existing Application Server, PeopleSoft Pure Internet Architecture (PIA), and Process
    Scheduler domains for the old release using the PSADMIN utility.
    See PeopleTools System and Server Administration, "Using PSADMIN Command-Line Interface."
2.  On Microsoft Windows, stop the services for the domains, as follows:
    a.  Open the Services dialog box, for example by clicking Start and selecting Administrative Tools, Services.
    b.  Right-click the PsftAppServerDomain<Appserver_domain_name>Service and select Stop.
    c.  Right-click the PsftPrcsDomain<ProcSched_domain_name>Service and select Stop.
    d.  Right-click the PsftPIADomain<PIA_domain_name>Service and select Stop.
    e.  Select File, Exit, to close the dialog box.
3.  Deploy PeopleTools Client for 8.62.xx on Microsoft Windows host using the PeopleTools Client DPK, as
    follows.
    a.  Download the PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62 DPK from My Oracle Support.
        Go to the PeopleTools Patches Home Page, My Oracle Support, Doc ID 2062712.2, and locate the link for
        the PeopleTools DPKs for the new release. The fourth zip file (for example, PEOPLETOOLS-WIN-
        8.62.xx_4of4.zip) includes the DPK for the PeopleTools Windows Client.
    b.  Install the PeopleTools 8.62.xx client using the fourth DPK zip file (PeopleTools Client DPK).
        See "Deploying the PeopleTools Client DPK," Deploying the PeopleTools Client DPK.
        Note. The PeopleTools-only Upgrade change package is delivered with the PeopleTools Windows Client
        DPK and the PeopleTools server DPKs.
    c.  During the deployment, select these options:
        Choose "PeopleTools Full Upgrade."
        Choose the option to install Change Assistant.
4.  Upgrade the PeopleTools database running on the existing (old) release.
    a.  Configure the database setup in Change Assistant for the database that needs to be upgraded.
        See PeopleTools Change Assistant and Update Manager, "Using Change Assistant to Upgrade
        PeopleTools."
        See the upgrade document Getting Started on Your PeopleTools Upgrade for your new PeopleTools
        release on the PeopleTools 8.62 Upgrade Home Page.
        See PeopleTools 8.62 Upgrade Home Page, My Oracle Support, Doc ID 3079786.1.
    b.  Follow the PeopleTools-only upgrade instructions for upgrading the database to the new PeopleTools
        release.
        See the PeopleTools Upgrade Documentation for your new PeopleTools release on the PeopleTools 8.62
        Upgrade Home Page.
5.  Deploy PS_HOME and create new domains using the PeopleTools 8.62.xx DPK with mid-tier option.
    Perform this step as referenced in the PeopleTools upgrade documentation.
    For this upgrade process, it is required that you use the component software that is installed by the
    PeopleTools DPK for Oracle Tuxedo, Oracle WebLogic, and Oracle Database Client. Do not use existing
    installations of Oracle Tuxedo, Oracle WebLogic, and Oracle Database Client.
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                               251
Performing a PeopleTools-Only Upgrade Using the PeopleSoft                                           Appendix B
PeopleTools DPKs
   Note. If the server where you plan to deploy PS_HOME and mid-tier components is a Microsoft Windows

   Note. Before deploying new domains against an existing database, review the default DPK settings, and

After you complete the steps above:
•  The PeopleTools database is upgraded to 8.62.xx.

•  The new PeopleTools 8.62.xx PS_HOME is created.
•  New Application Server, PIA, and Process Scheduler domains are created connecting to the database
   upgraded to PeopleTools 8.62.xx.

 Appendix C
Installing PeopleSoft Change Assistant
This appendix discusses:
 •  Understanding PeopleSoft Change Assistant
 •  Using the Change Assistant Setup Script to Install, Upgrade, or Uninstall
 •  Configuring and Using PeopleSoft Change Assistant
 •  Validating Change Assistant Settings
Understanding PeopleSoft Change Assistant
 Oracle's PeopleSoft Change Assistant is a standalone tool, provided with PeopleSoft PeopleTools, that enables
 you to assemble and organize the steps necessary to apply patches and fixes for maintenance updates as well as
 perform PeopleSoft upgrades. You use different modes of Change Assistant to carry out maintenance both for
 PeopleSoft applications using the PeopleSoft Update Manager, and those using the classic patching method.
 To take advantage of all of the Change Assistant features, you must install on a Microsoft Windows-based
 operating system. On Linux, you can use a command-line version of Change Assistant, and run in Update
 Manager mode only.
 When you run the setup script for the PeopleTools Client DPK, you can choose to install Change Assistant as part
 of the client installation. If you need to install, upgrade, or remove Change Assistant independently of the
 PeopleTools Client DPK deployment, use the instructions in this chapter.
 See "Deploying the PeopleTools Client DPK."
 For the current PeopleSoft PeopleTools release, the Change Assistant installation includes the following features:
 •  You can run the setup.bat script located in PS_HOME\setup\PsCA to install, update, or remove Change
    Assistant.
    The PS_HOME/setup/PsCA directory includes setup.bat and a sample response file, resp_file.txt. The
    PS_HOME/setup/PsCA folder is available in both the PeopleTools Client DPK deployment and PeopleTools
    DPK server deployments.
    Note. The PS_HOME\setup\PsCA directory includes scripts and files that are reserved for use by the software.
    You can ignore these files, including silentInstall.bat and CA-silentInstall-response-file.txt.
 •  You must run from PS_HOME/setup/PsCA.
    Do not copy the PsCA folder alone to another location and try to install.
 •  You can specify the installation location on the command line, in response to the script prompts, or in a text
    file.
    If you do not supply the installation location, the script prompts you for the necessary information. To upgrade
    or remove an existing installation, you run the script with no options, and the script prompts you for the
    necessary information.
 Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                               253
 Installing PeopleSoft Change Assistant                                                  Appendix C
 •  You can install multiple instances of Change Assistant from the current release on one physical machine.
    Note. Multiple instances of Change Assistant can run in parallel on the same machine. However, when
    configuring Change Assistant, for example to set up update or upgrade jobs, you cannot run multiple instances
    against the same target database. That is, you cannot specify the same target database on different Change
    Assistant instances.
    See PeopleTools: Change Assistant and Update Manager, "Running Multiple Instances of Change Assistant."
 •  You must remove installations of Change Assistant from PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.55 or earlier before
    installing from the current release. Change Assistant from PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62 cannot coexist with
    that from earlier releases.
 •  You must install each Change Assistant instance in a separate installation location.
 •  You can remove or upgrade each Change Assistant instance separately.
 •  When you remove an installation instance, you have the option to save the existing configuration information
    in a group of files gathered in a zip archive. You can configure Change Assistant at a later time by importing
    the zip file.
 For more information on using Change Assistant for updates and for software upgrades, see the PeopleSoft
 product documentation.
 See Also
 PeopleTools: Change Assistant and Update Manager
 PeopleTools: Application Designer Lifecycle Management Guide
Task C-1: Using the Change Assistant Setup Script to Install,
Upgrade, or Uninstall
This section discusses:
 •  Running the Change Assistant Setup Script with Command-Line Parameters
 •  Running the Change Assistant Setup Script with a Response File
 •  Running the Change Assistant Setup Script Interactively to Install
 •  Running the Change Assistant Setup Script Interactively to Upgrade
 •  Running the Change Assistant Setup Script Interactively to Uninstall
 •  Using Change Assistant on Linux
 254                                                                        Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix C                                                                                 Installing PeopleSoft Change Assistant
Task C-1-1: Running the Change Assistant Setup Script with Command-
Line Parameters
You can use the following command-line parameters with the Change Assistant setup script.
 Command-line Parameter                                             Description
 -p <installation_path>                                           Supply the full path for the Change Assistant installation
                                                                  location.
                                                                  If the location includes spaces, surround it with double
                                                                  quotes, such as "C:\PS\Change Assistant".
 -t <action_type>                                                 Use one of the following value for <action_type>.
                                                                •   NEW or new
                                                                    Create a new Change Assistant instance.
                                                                •   UPGRADE or upgrade
                                                                    Upgrade an existing instance that was installed from the
                                                                    current PeopleSoft PeopleTools release.
                                                                •   UNINSTALL or uninstall
                                                                    Remove an existing Change Assistant instance.
 -bkp <backup or no backup>                                       Use one of these values:
                                                                •   BACKUP or backup
                                                                    Create a zip file containing files with configuration
                                                                    information. The backup file, changeassistantcfgbak.zip,
                                                                    is saved in the installation location.
                                                                •   NOBACKUP or nobackup
                                                                    Do not create a backup file with the configuration
                                                                    information.
 -lp <log_file_location>                                          Specify the full path where you want the log file to be saved.
To use the Change Assistant setup script with command-line options:
1.  Open a command prompt and change directory to PS_HOME\setup\PsCA.
2.  Enter the following command to specify the installation location for a new installation.
    For <installation_path>, supply the full path to the location where you want to install Change Assistant.
    setup.bat -p <installation_path> -t new
    For example:
    setup.bat -p "C:\PS\Change Assistant" -t new
3.  If you want to specify the installation location and a location for the log file, enter the following command.
    For <installation_path>, supply the full path to the location where you want to install Change Assistant. For
    <log_file_location>, supply the full path to the location for the installation log file.
    setup.bat -p <installation_path> -lp <log_file_location> -t new
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                                              255
Installing PeopleSoft Change Assistant                                                               Appendix C
4.  If you want to upgrade an existing installation, enter the following command:
    setup.bat -t upgrade
5.  If you want to remove an existing installation, and save a backup, enter the following command:
    setup.bat -t uninstall -bkp backup
Task C-1-2: Running the Change Assistant Setup Script with a Response
File
To use the Change Assistant setup script with the installation location in a text file:
1.  Open the sample response file PS_HOME\setup\PsCA\resp_file.txt for editing, for example in Notepad.
2.  Modify the parameter INSTALL_PATH="C:\PS\Change Assistant" to specify the desired
    installation path.
    If the installation location includes spaces, enclose it in double quotes. Save the file.
3.  Open a command prompt and change directory to PS_HOME\setup\PsCA.
4.  Run this command:
    setup.bat -f resp_file.txt
Task C-1-3: Running the Change Assistant Setup Script Interactively to
Install

 Task C-1-6: Using Change Assistant on Linux
 You can use a command-line version of Change Assistant on Linux for Update Manager mode only. Change
 Assistant on Linux can not be used for target databases that are below PeopleTools 8.55.16.
 To install Change Assistant on Linux:
 1.  In a terminal window, change directory to PS_HOME/setup/PsCA.
 2.  Verify that you are running as a user with permission for the files in PsCA.
     For example, if your installation includes the default Linux users set up by the DPK, run as the psadm1 user.
     See Using the PeopleSoft Installation.
 3.  Run the setup script and specify the installation directory.
     $ ./setup.sh -p /ds1/changeassistant -t new
 4.  Go to the installation directory, and run Change Assistant in Update Manager mode using the command-line
     options in the Change Assistant product documentation.
     See PeopleTools: Change Assistant and Update Manager, "Running Change Assistant Job from the
     Command Line."

PeopleSoft Update Manager (PUM) Home Page, My Oracle Support, Doc ID 1641843.2
Task C-2-3: Scanning the Workstation
The first time you use PeopleSoft Change Assistant, it automatically scans your workstation for applications that
it will use in order to automate the steps. For example, it automatically finds the SQL Query tool and uses it to run
SQL commands or scripts.
If you add a new application or update an existing application, PeopleSoft Change Assistant must perform a scan
of the system in order to discover the changes. To perform this scan, select Tools, Scan Configuration.
Task C-2-4: Exporting Jobs to XML, HTML, or Microsoft Excel Format
Change Assistant allows users to export jobs to XML, HTML, or Microsoft Excel file formats. Do this by
selecting File, Export Job in Change Assistant. Then, enter the desired exported filename and select the desired
file type format.
Task C-3: Validating Change Assistant Settings
After you have set up and configured PeopleSoft Change Assistant and the Environment Management
components, you should validate your PeopleSoft Change Assistant and environment settings.
PeopleSoft Change Assistant validates settings by:
•  Locating valid SQL query tools required to run SQL scripts.
•  Testing the Environment Management hub and ensuring that PeopleSoft Change Assistant can communicate
   with it.
•  Testing My Oracle Support and ensuring that PeopleSoft Change Assistant can communicate with it.
   PeopleSoft Change Assistant sends a ping to My Oracle Support and then tests the connection. In order for the
   validation to succeed, the machine where you have PeopleSoft Change Assistant installed must have the ping
   feature enabled.
You can also print a summary of your environment, which can facilitate the diagnosis of problems by Oracle
Software Support.
To validate your environment, select Tools, Options, Validate. Click Start Validation.

 Appendix D
Encrypting Passwords for Customization
Files
This appendix discusses:
 •   Encrypting Passwords for Customization Files on Linux, AIX, or Solaris
 •   Encrypting Passwords for Customization Files on Microsoft Windows
Task D-1: Encrypting Passwords for Customization Files on
Linux, AIX, or Solaris
 This section describes how to produce an encrypted version of a clear text password and include it in the
 psft_customizations.yaml file for a customized deployment for non-default users and groups on Linux, AIX, or
 Solaris. This section applies to installations with the Native OS for Linux, AIX, or Solaris DPKs.
 Note. You can skip this section if you are creating psft_customizations.yaml files for silent mode installations.
 Use clear text passwords with silent mode installations.
 In general, when you run the DPK setup script, you supply several user IDs and passwords, such as the Connect
 ID password and operator ID password. The script encrypts the passwords that you supply and includes them in
 the generated YAML files in BASE_DIR/dpk/puppet/production/data. When you create a
 psft_customizations.yaml file, you can copy these encrypted passwords from the generated YAML files and
 include them in the psft_customizations.yaml file. However, the passwords for the Linux, AIX, or Solaris users
 are not prompted for, and therefore the encrypted passwords are not available in any of the generated YAML files.
 Note that the successful use of the encrypted password depends on the presence of the public and private keys in
 the BASE_DIR/dpk/puppet directory referred to in the eyaml encrypt command. You cannot save an encrypted
 password and use it with a deployment with a different BASE_DIR.
 This procedure assumes that you have carried out the first portion of a customized deployment, and stopped at the
 question "Do you want to continue with the default initialization?"
 See "Completing the DPK Initialization with Customizations," Preparing the Customization Files for Linux, AIX,
 or Solaris Users and Groups.
 To encrypt a password:
 1.  Run this command to source the pspuppet.sh script, which sets necessary environment variables (the
     command begins with a period followed by a space):
     . BASE_DIR/psft_puppet_agent/pspuppet.sh
     See "Completing the DPK Initialization with Customizations," Understanding the PeopleSoft Environment
     Customizations.
 2.  Open the BASE_DIR/dpk/puppet/hiera.yaml file and note the full path to the public and private keys:
 Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                                   263
Encrypting Passwords for Customization Files                                                            Appendix D
    :pkcs7_private_key: BASE_DIR/dpk/puppet/secure/keys/private_⇒
    key.pkcs7.pem
    :pkcs7_public_key:          BASE_DIR/dpk/puppet/secure/keys/public_key.pkcs7.pem
3.  Run the following command in a terminal window, supplying the paths from the previous step:
    <BASE_DIR>/psft_puppet_agent/bin/eyaml encrypt -s "<clear_password>" --⇒
    pkcs7-private-key=<private_key_full_path>/<key_name> --pkcs7-public-⇒
    key=<public_key_full_path>/<key_name> --output=string
    Note. The double-quotes around the password are required. Be sure to use the correct punctuation for the
    command. Note that the command uses different punctuation for the keys than the hiera.yaml file.
    Note. If your password includes an exclamation mark (!) you must enclose the entire password in double
    quotes surrounded by single quotes. For example, '"passw!ord"'.
    Example for Linux:
    BASE_DIR/psft_puppet_agent/bin/eyaml encrypt -s "password" --pkcs7-⇒
    private-key=/cs1/psft/dpk/puppet/secure/keys/private_key.pkcs7.pem --⇒
    pkcs7-public-key=/cs1/psft/dpk/puppet/secure/keys/public_key.pkcs7.pem⇒
    --output=string
    Example for AIX or Solaris:
    BASE_DIR/psft_puppet_agent/bin/eyaml encrypt -s "password" --pkcs7-⇒
    private-key=/cs1/psft/dpk/puppet/secure/keys/private_key.pkcs7.pem --⇒
    pkcs7-public-key=/cs1/psft/dpk/puppet/secure/keys/public_key.pkcs7.pem⇒
    --output=string
4.  Copy the encrypted password from the output in the terminal window.
    The encrypted text will be a long single line of letters and numbers. Be sure to copy the text in one unbroken
    line, with no spaces or line feeds. Here is a truncated representation of an encrypted password:
    ENC[PKCS7,MIIBeQYJKoZIhvc...................]
5.  Paste the encrypted password in the psft_customizations.yaml file, replacing the text password.
    Again, the encrypted text must be a single line. Also, be sure to retain the indentation in the
    psft_customizations.yaml file. This is a sample psft_customizations.yaml for a new single user and existing
    single group:
    ---
    psft_runtime_user_name: newusr3
    users:
     psft_user:
        name: newusr3
        gid: 35000
        home_dir: /dpk_base/home/userhome
        password: ENC[PKCS7,MIIBeQYJKoZIhvc...................]
        remove: false
6.  Use the psft_customizations.yaml file for deployment with the psft_puppet_apply script.
264                                                                         Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix D                                                             Encrypting Passwords for Customization Files
    The DPK deployment will automatically decrypt the password from the psft_customizations.yaml and use it
    for deployment.
Task D-2: Encrypting Passwords for Customization Files on
Microsoft Windows
This section describes how to produce an encrypted version of a clear text password and include it in the
psft_customizations.yaml file for a customized deployment on Microsoft Windows. This section applies to
installations with the Native OS for Windows DPKs.
Note. You can skip this section if you are creating psft_customizations.yaml files for silent mode installations.
Use clear text passwords with silent mode installations.
In general, when you run the DPK setup script, you supply several user IDs and passwords, such as the Connect
ID password and operator ID password. The script encrypts the passwords that you supply and includes them in
the generated YAML files in BASE_DIR/dpk/puppet/production/data. When you create a
psft_customizations.yaml file, you can copy these encrypted passwords from the generated YAML files and
include them in the psft_customizations.yaml file. If you need to encrypt a different password, you can use this
procedure.
Note that the successful use of the encrypted password depends on the presence of the public and private keys in
the BASE_DIR/dpk/puppet directory referred to in the eyaml encrypt command. You cannot save an encrypted
password and use it with a deployment with a different BASE_DIR.
This procedure assumes that you have carried out the first portion of a customized deployment, and stopped at the
question "Do you want to continue with the default initialization?"
See "Completing the DPK Initialization with Customizations."
To encrypt a password:
1.  Open the BASE_DIR\dpk\puppet\hiera.yaml file and note the full path to the public and private keys:
    :pkcs7_private_key: BASE_DIR\dpk\puppet\secure\keys\private_⇒
    key.pkcs7.pem
    :pkcs7_public_key: BASE_DIR\dpk\puppet\secure\keys\public_key.pkcs7.pem
2.  Open a command prompt and run the following command, supplying the full paths from the previous step.
    Enter the command on a single line.
    <BASE_DIR>\psft_puppet_agent\bin\eyaml encrypt -s "<clear_password>" --⇒
    pkcs7-private-key=<private_key_full_path>\<key_name> --pkcs7-public-⇒
    key=<public_key_full_path>\<key_name> --output=string
    Note. The double-quotes around the password are required. Be sure to use the correct punctuation for the
    command. Note that the command uses different punctuation for the keys than the hiera.yaml file.
    Note. If your password includes an exclamation mark (!) you must enclose the entire password in double
    quotes surrounded by single quotes. For example, '"passw!ord"'.
    For example:
    C:\psft\psft_puppet_agent\bin\eyaml encrypt -s "password" --pkcs7-⇒
    private-key=C:\psft\dpk\puppet\secure\keys\private_key.pkcs7.pem --⇒
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                                   265
Encrypting Passwords for Customization Files                                                              Appendix D
    pkcs7-public-key=C:\psft\dpk\puppet\secure\keys\public_key.pkcs7.pem --⇒
    output=string
3.  Copy the encrypted password from the output in the command prompt window.
    The encrypted text will be a long single line of letters and numbers. Be sure to copy the text in one unbroken
    line, with no spaces or line feeds. Here is a truncated representation of an encrypted password:
    ENC[PKCS7,MIIBeQYJKoZIhvc...................]
4.  Paste the encrypted password in the psft_customizations.yaml file, replacing the text password.
    Again, the encrypted text must be a single line. Also, be sure to retain the indentation in the
    psft_customizations.yaml file. This is a sample psft_customizations.yaml with an entry for the connect ID:
    ---
    db_connect_id:      people
    db_connect_pwd:     ENC[PKCS7,MIIBeQYJKoZIhvc...................]
5.  Use the psft_customizations.yaml file for deployment with the psft_puppet_apply script.
    The DPK deployment will automatically decrypt the password from the psft_customizations.yaml and use it
    for deployment.

 Appendix E
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers
This appendix discusses:
 •  Understanding Container Support for PeopleSoft DPKs
 •  Prerequisites for Container Deployment
 •  Configuring the Linux VM for Container Deployment
 •  Building and Running the Container for Mid-tier Deployment
 •  Building and Running the Container for Search Components
 •  Working With PeopleSoft Containers
Understanding Container Support for PeopleSoft DPKs
 The current PeopleSoft PeopleTools release includes support for deploying PeopleSoft environments in
 containers. Containers have the advantage of isolating your environment, so that the VM or computer running it
 does not have to be changed. In addition, containers provide a standard environment, which aids in
 troubleshooting and support.
 The container setup process makes use of the PeopleSoft DPKs, setup script, and silent installation files, along
 with a new API and container-specific scripts, to enable you to build Podman containers. The following
 deployments are currently supported:
 •  Mid-tier — Deploy an application server, Process Scheduler, and web server (PIA) that connects to an
    existing PeopleSoft application database.
    See PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62 Deployment Packages Installation, "Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in
    Containers."
 •  Deploy an Application server, Process Scheduler, or web server (PIA).
    Deploy each component in a separate container.
    See PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62 Deployment Packages Installation, "Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in
    Containers."
 •  Search components — Deploy OpenSearch, OpenSearch Dashboards, and Logstash.
    After building and running search containers, you have the option to run Automated Configuration
    Management (ACM) plug-ins to integrate OpenSearch with the PeopleSoft environment.
    See PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62 Deployment Packages Installation, "Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in
    Containers."
 •  Mid-tier and Search components — Deploy container images on Oracle Kubernetes Engine.
    See PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62 Deployment Packages Installation, "Deploying PeopleSoft Containers on
    Oracle Kubernetes Engine."
 Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                                  267
 Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                              Appendix E
 •  Full-tier (PUM) — Deploy the PeopleSoft Update Images to create a PeopleSoft Update Manager (PUM)
    source for applying updates and fixes.
    The full-tier installation sets up a PeopleSoft application database on Oracle Database Server, application
    server, Process Scheduler, and web server (PIA).
    See PeopleSoft Deployment Packages for Update Images Installation (PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62),
    "Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers."
 •  Full-tier (FRESH) — Deploy a full-tier PeopleSoft environment with the FRESH installation type.
    See PeopleSoft 9.2 Application Installation for Oracle (PeopleSoft PeopleTools 8.62), "Deploying PeopleSoft
    DPKs in Containers."
 If you want to acquire updated components or CPUs for PeopleSoft environments deployed in containers, the
 recommended approach is to use the latest PeopleSoft DPKs to create new containers. This will create
 environments with the latest available CPUs for Oracle WebLogic, Oracle Tuxedo, Java, and Oracle Database
 Client. You can then create container images from the new containers. Be aware that the use of PT-INFRA DPK
 to apply CPUs on an existing container environment is not supported.
Prerequisites for Container Deployment
 Before beginning the deployment, you must fulfill the following requirements:
 •  Access to My Oracle Support to download the DPKs
 •  Access to Oracle Container Registry
    The Oracle Linux 8 image for the installation will be downloaded from this location during the container build
    process.
    See Oracle Container Registry, https://container-registry.oracle.com.
 •  An Oracle Linux VM
    The Oracle Linux VM is the host for the container, and must be configured to use Podman as described in this
    documentation.
 •  Podman and podman-compose
    Each user that builds and runs containers and the prebuilt container images must be able to run Podman and
    podman-compose. The task Configuring the Linux VM for Container Deployment includes steps to verify that
    Podman and podman-compose are available.
 •  A non-root user with sudo root permission
    To configure the Linux VM, build and run containers, the user must be able to use the sudo command.
 •  The PeopleSoft container deployment supports these versions:
    •  Oracle Linux Server release 8.7
    •  Red Hat Enterprise Linux release 8.7 (Ootpa)
    •  podman version 4.9
    •  podman-compose version 1.0.6 (optional)
Task E-1: Configuring the Linux VM for Container Deployment
This section discusses:
 268                                                                           Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.

Task E-1-1: Setting Proxy Environment Variables
Ensure that the following proxy-related environment variables are configured with the correct values for your
environment, such as server name and port for the proxy server, if necessary.
export http_proxy=<proxy value>
export https_proxy=<proxy value>
export HTTP_PROXY=<proxy value>
export HTTPS_PROXY=<proxy value>
export no_proxy=<proxy value>
Task E-1-2: Installing Podman
Run the following command to install Podman on the Linux VM.
Use these commands to build a container image and to run containers:
sudo dnf module info container-tools
  sudo dnf -y module install container-tools:ol8 && sudo dnf clean all
Use these commands to run a container:
sudo dnf info podman
sudo dnf -y install podman && sudo dnf clean all
Use this command to create a nodocker file. This is used by the Podman build.
sudo touch "/etc/containers/nodocker"
Use these commands to verify the Podman version:
podman info
podman -v
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                               269
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                                     Appendix E
Task E-1-3: Installing Podman-compose
Similarly to Podman, podman-compose supports the same command line options as compose. It also supports
reading compose configuration files that are delivered with the PeopleSoft container files, such as compose.yaml,
compose-pum.yaml, and compose-osk.yaml. This means that these configuration files can be interchangeable
between compose and podman-compose, typically without any modification.
To install podman-compose, refer to the Oracle tutorial.
See Use Compose Files with Podman on Oracle Linux, Install Podman Compose,
https://docs.oracle.com/en/learn/ol-podman-compose/index.html#install-podman-compose.
Task E-1-4: Setting Up Podman in Rootless Mode
Run the following commands to authorize users and group IDs.
echo "${USER}:231072:65536" | sudo tee -a                       "/etc/subuid"
echo "${USER}:231072:65536" | sudo tee -a                       "/etc/subgid"
/usr/bin/systemctl --user daemon-reload
podman system migrate
Task E-1-5: Setting Up the Storage Configuration File
The storage requirement for PeopleTools mid-tier containers is 15 GB or greater. The storage requirement for
PeopleSoft Update Image container is 76 GB or greater.
Edit the file at /etc/containers/storage.conf to configure the system-wide storage. The default location
$HOME/.local/share/containers/storage defined in /etc/containers/storage.conf` may not have sufficient storage
space for containers.
The examples in this section use /scratch/podman-rs/storage as the Podman storage path and /scratch/podman-
rs/storage/tmp as the default location for storing temporary container image content. The file
/etc/containers/containers.conf includes instructions for changing the default storage location for Podman by
modifying the TMPDIR environment variable.
Use these steps to set up the storage configuration file, storage.conf.
Note. Enter each command on a single line. Do not include the line-continuation arrow (⇒).
1.  Create the storage directory.
    mkdir -p "/scratch/podman-rs/storage"
2.  Create a directory for temporary container image content.
    mkdir -p "/scratch/podman-rs/storage/tmp"
3.  Change the storage location to /scratch/podman-rs/storage..
    sudo sed -i 's|# rootless_storage_path = "$HOME/.local/share/containers⇒
    /storage"|rootless_storage_path = "/scratch/podman-rs/storage"|g' "/etc⇒
    /containers/storage.conf"
270                                                                      Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix E                                                                 Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers
4.  Verify that the storage path for rootless users points to /scratch/podman-rs/storage.
    podman info | grep graphRoot
Task E-1-6: Setting Up the Container Configuration File
Use these steps to set up the container configuration file.
Note. Enter each command on a single line. Do not include the line-continuation arrow (⇒).
1.  Copy the default container configuration file to a new file for your configuration.
    sudo cp "/usr/share/containers/containers.conf" "/etc/containers"
2.  Change the default networking for Podman to netavark.
    sudo sed -i 's|network_backend = "cni"|network_backend = "netavark"/g'⇒
     "/etc/containers/containers.conf"
3.  Set the timeout.
    sudo sed -i 's|#stop_timeout = 10|stop_timeout = 90|g' "/etc/containers⇒
    /containers.conf"
4.  Set the temporary directory.
    sudo sed -i 's|# image_copy_tmp_dir="/var/tmp"|image_copy_tmp_dir=⇒
    "storage"|g'      "/etc/containers/containers.conf"
Task E-1-7: Running Additional Setup Commands
Run these other setup commands:
Enable linger to prevent systemd from shutting down your containers when you log out.
sudo loginctl enable-linger "$(whoami)"
Task E-1-8: Verifying Podman
To confirm that Podman works, enter one of these commands:
podman run --rm hello-world
Or:
podman run --rm quay.io/podman/hello

Podman Installation Instructions, https://podman.io/docs/installation
Oracle Podman User's Guide, https://docs.oracle.com/en/operating-systems/oracle-linux/podman/toc.htm
Podman tutorial on GitHub, https://github.com/containers/podman/blob/main/docs/tutorials/rootless_tutorial.md

Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                               Appendix E
Podman Troubleshooting Guide on GitHub, https://github.com/containers/podman/blob/main/troubleshooting.md
Task E-1-9: Specifying sysctl Settings
Modify the /etc/sysctl.conf file for the DPK deployment. The sysctl settings will be inherited by the containers.
sh
sudo tee -a "/etc/sysctl.conf" <<EOF
# sysctl setting for PeopleTools
kernel.msgmnb = 65538
kernel.msgmni = 1024
kernel.msgmax = 65536
kernel.shmmax = 68719476736
kernel.shmall = 4294967296
kernel.core_uses_pid = 1
net.ipv4.tcp_keepalive_time = 90
net.ipv4.tcp_timestamps = 1
net.ipv4.tcp_window_scaling = 1
net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range = 10000 65500
# sysctl setting for PeopleTools search
vm.max_map_count=262144
net.core.somaxconn=1024
net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start = 80
EOF
sudo /usr/sbin/sysctl -p
Task E-1-10: Setting Environment Variables
Proxy variables are used to pull an Oracle Linux image from Oracle Container Registry. Building PeopleTools
container images using Podman is similar to Docker. These additional environment variables are required for
proper functioning.
•  This variable is required to support HEALTHCHECK specified in the Containerfile:
   export BUILDAH_FORMAT=docker
•  This variable is required while building the container image in rootless mode.
   export BUILDAH_ISOLATION=chroot
Run this command to add the necessary environment variables to the .bash_profile file for the user that will run
the containers. Substitute the proxy values in your environment for each <..>.
tee -a ~/.bash_profile <<EOF
export http_proxy=<..>
export https_proxy=<..>
export HTTP_PROXY=<..>
export HTTPS_PROXY=<..>
export no_proxy=<..>
export BUILDAH_FORMAT=docker
export BUILDAH_ISOLATION=chroot
export TMPDIR=/scratch/tmp
EOF

 Appendix E                                                                    Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers
 Task E-1-11: Verifying Access to Oracle Container Registry
 The user that will build and run the container should perform these steps to verify they can obtain the necessary
 images from Oracle Container Registry (OCR): This can be a non-root user.
 1.  Log in to Oracle Container Registry with Podman.
     podman login container-registry.oracle.com
 2.  Test pulling an Oracle Linux 8 image.
     podman pull container-registry.oracle.com/os/oraclelinux:8
Task E-2: Building and Running the Container for Mid-tier
Deployment
This section discusses:
 •   Using the DPKs to Install PS_HOME
 •   Preparing to Deploy the PeopleTools Mid-tier Container
 •   Building the Container Image for PeopleTools Mid-tier
 •   Running the Container Image for PeopleTools Mid-tier
 •   Starting and Stopping the PeopleTools Mid-tier Container
 •   Removing the PeopleTools Mid-tier Container
 Task E-2-1: Using the DPKs to Install PS_HOME
 Follow the instructions in this documentation to obtain the PeopleSoft PeopleTools DPKs and install PS_HOME.
 1.  Obtain the four PeopleSoft PeopleTools DPKs for Native OS Linux from My Oracle Support.
     See "Deploying the PeopleSoft PeopleTools Deployment Packages," Obtaining the PeopleSoft PeopleTools
     DPKs.
 2.  Install PS_HOME on the Linux VM.
     See "Deploying the PeopleSoft PeopleTools Deployment Packages," Running the DPK Setup Script to Install
     PS_HOME Only.
 3.  Locate the containers directory.
     The PS_HOME/containers/OraclePeopleTools directory includes text instructions, scripts, and configuration
     files. You will use these files in the following sections to build and run containers.
     Note. You can view README.md and FAQ.md in a text editor. The .md extension refers to the Markdown
     language used for GitHub documents.
     Note. You can install PS_HOME on a computer and copy the OraclePeopleTools directory to the Linux VM
     where you want to build and run containers.
 4.  Set the CONTAINER_HOME environment variable to the location of the OraclePeopleTools directory that
     holds the container files in your environment.
 Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                      273
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                          Appendix E
    This documentation uses ${CONTAINER_HOME} in code samples to refer to this location.
    export CONTAINER_HOME=<Path to Container Code directory OraclePeople⇒
    Tools>
    For example, if you installed PS_HOME in the directory /opt/oracle/ps_home8.62:
    export CONTAINER_HOME=/opt/oracle/ps_home8.62/containers/OraclePeople⇒
    Tools
The PS_HOME/containers/OraclePeopleTools directory includes detailed README.md files for various
deployments. Refer to these README.md files for current information. Note that the file structure and directory
names may differ in different PeopleTools releases.
•   PS_HOME/containers/OraclePeopleTools/README.md — Describes the initial setup of Podman on a Linux
    VM.
•   PS_HOME/containers/OraclePeopleTools/containerfiles/pt/README.md — Describes the procedure to build
    and deploy PeopleTools mid-tier containers on a Linux VM.
•   PS_HOME/containers/OraclePeopleTools/containerfiles/osk/README.md — Describes the process to build
    and deploy search containers on a Linux VM.
•   PS_HOME/containers/OraclePeopleTools/k8s/README.md — Describes the process to deploy PeopleTools
    mid-tier and search containers on a Kubernetes cluster (OKE).
•   PS_HOME/containers/OraclePeopleTools/helm-charts/oracle-peopletools/README.md — Describes the
    process to deploy PeopleTools mid-tier and search containers on a Kubernetes cluster (OKE) with Helm.
Task E-2-2: Preparing to Deploy the PeopleTools Mid-tier Container
You can use the PeopleTools DPKs to create and run the following containers.
•   Application server, Process Scheduler, and web server (PIA) (mid-tier)
•   Application server (appserver)
•   Process Scheduler (prcs)
•   Application server and Process Scheduler (appbatch)
•   Web server (PIA)
The image build process creates an image by installing PeopleTools DPK in silent and deploy_only mode. To
prepare to deploy PeopleTools mid-tier containers:
1.  Review the instructions in CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/pt/README.md.
2.  Copy the four PeopleSoft PeopleTools DPKs zip files into CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/pt.
    Don't unzip the files. Don't use symbolic links.
3.  If you want to use the PT-INFRA DPKs to apply update CPUs, copy the DPK zip files into
    CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/pt.
    Don't unzip the files. Don't use symbolic links.
4.  Review CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/pt/install.rsp.
    The install.rsp file is the silent installation response file. This file is called by the silent installation command,
    which is contained in install.file in the same directory. The DPK deploy_only option installs the software
    required for the mid-tier components without setting up the mid-tier domains.
    env_type=midtier
274                                                                           Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix E                                                                  Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers
    deploy_only=True
    db_platform=ORACLE
    db_is_unicode=True
    psft_base_dir=/opt/oracle
    user_home_dir=/home
5.  Review the installation command in CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/pt/install.file, but do not make any
    changes.
    The command deploys the DPK in silent installation mode with a customization file.
    psft-dpk-setup.sh --silent --response_file=/in/pt/install.rsp --⇒
    customization_file=/in/pt/psft_customizations.yaml --oke_build
6.  Review the information in CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/pt/psft_customizations.yaml.
    The psft_customizations.yaml file includes the only DPK customizations that are supported for containers.
    # setup_share is not supported in containers
    setup_share:              false
    # setup_sysctl is not supported in containers
    setup_sysctl:             false
    Do not change the setup_share and setup_sysctl parameters.
    The parameter setup_sysctl: false specifies that the DPK setup will not overwrite Linux system
    parameters. The parameter setup_share: false specifies that the DPK setup does not create a share for
    access to the Linux VM.
Task E-2-3: Building the Container Image for PeopleTools Mid-tier
To build the container image:
1.  Change directory to the directory housing the container code.
    cd ${CONTAINER_HOME}/containerfiles/pt
2.  Set the BUILDAH_FORMAT environment variable, which is required to support HEALTHCHECK specified
    in the Containerfile.
    export BUILDAH_FORMAT=docker
3.  Set the BUILDAH_ISOLATION environment variable, which is required for building the container image in
    rootless mode.
    export BUILDAH_ISOLATION=chroot
4.  Run this command to build the image:
    podman build --no-cache -f ./Containerfile -t [image_name:tag] . | tee⇒
     bldimg.log
    For [image_name:tag] substitute a tagged name to apply to the built image. Use lower-case letters for the
    name.
    If building in a local VM, use this format: oracle/peopletools/deployonly:8.6x.
    If you are planning to push the container image to Oracle Container Registry (OCR), use this format: <region-
    key>.ocir.io/<oci tenancy>/oracle/peopletools/deployonly:8.6x
    See Preparing for Container Registry, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                               275
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                                    Appendix E
    us/iaas/Content/Registry/Concepts/registryprerequisites.htm.
    The image build process is completed when the build output shows "Successfully tagged
    localhost/<[image_name:tag]>" followed by the long image id.
5.  If you want to push the container image to OCR, use this command:
    podman push[image_name:tag]
6.  The build generates intermediate images of large size. Remove these images using following commands.
    podman images -a
    podman rmi $(podman images -a | grep "<none>.*<none>" | awk '{ print⇒
      $3; }') 2>/dev/null
    podman images -a
7.  Use these commands to get information on the image:
    podman images -a
    podman inspect <[image_name:tag]>
8.  In case the deployment results in errors, you can find information on error messages associated with the
    PeopleSoft containers in CONTAINER_HOME/FAQ.md.
Task E-2-4: Running the Container Image for PeopleTools Mid-tier
This section describes two options to run the container image. Use the podman command and define the setup
with command parameters, or edit a compose.yaml file and run a podman-compose command.
For both options, first ensure that the CONTAINER_HOME environment variable is set to the location of the
OraclePeopleTools directory that holds the container files in your environment.
export CONTAINER_HOME=<Path to Container Code directory OraclePeopleTools>
Option 1: To run the container image using the podman command:
1.  Change directory to CONTAINER_HOME/in/pt.
    cd ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/pt
    Note. The volume ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/pt should be mapped to container at /in/pt:ro. Use /in/pt:z if
    you are using shared volume/storage.
2.  Run this command:
    podman run -d --name <container name> \
    --hostname <container hostname> \
    -e DB_WAIT_TIMEOUT=1 \
    -p <Add Container Port Mapping> \
    -v [<host mount point>:]/in/pt \
    <[image_name:tag]>
    Use these guidelines for the command parameters:
    •  --name
       (Optional): The name of the container. If no name is entered, it will be auto generated by default.
276                                                                         Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix E                                                                  Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers
   •  --hostname
      The parameter <container hostname> will be used as the host name for the container. PeopleTools uses the
      hostname for various processes, so it is important to set the hostname properly.
      The container hostname must not contain any underscores ( _ ).
      Do not use the FQDN of the VM host or machine where the container runs for <container hostname>. For
      example, if your mid-tier container VM host or machine FQDN is server.example.one.oraclevcn.com, then
      set <container hostname> as mt.oraclevcn.com.
      After setting the hostname parameter, you would receive these results from queries in the container:
      hostname -f to mt.oraclevcn.com — container long host name (FQDN)
      hostname -d to oraclevcn.com — container DNS domain name
      hostname -a to mt — container alias name
      PeopleTools Auth Token Domain — [.oraclevcn.com]
      PIA URL will be http://server.example.one.oraclevcn.com:8000
      Use the hostname to determine the type of mid-tier deployment.
               Type of Container                                  Hostname
              Mid-tier (mt)                                       mt.oraclevcn.com
              Application server (appserver)                      appserver.oraclevcn.com
              Process Scheduler (prcs)                            prcs.oraclevcn.com
              Application server and Process Scheduler (appbatch) appbatch.oraclevcn.com
              Web server (PIA)                                    pia.oraclevcn.com
   •  -e
      (Optional): Environment variable indicating the number of seconds to wait for the database.
      DB_WAIT_TIMEOUT=<wait time for DB in seconds>t
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                              277
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                     Appendix E
    •  -p
       The port mapping of the host port to the container port, in the format < host ports >:< fixed container ports
       >.
       Ensure that the ports are not in use. Each container must use different ports.
              Container Type                                     Port Mapping String
             mid-tier                                           -p 10100:10100 -p 10101:10101 -p 7000-7003:7000-
                                                                7003 -p 7010:7010 -p 8000:8000 -p 8001:8001
             appserver                                          -p 10100:10100 -p 10101:10101 -p 7000-7003:7000-
                                                                7003 -p 7010:7010 -p 9033-9043:9033-9043
             prcs                                               NA
             appbatch                                           -p 10100:10100 -p 10101:10101 -p 7000-7003:7000-
                                                                7003 -p 7010:7010 -p 9033-9043:9033-9043
             pia                                                -p 8000:8000 -p 8001:8001
    •  -v
       Enter one of the following for the host mount point:
       ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/pt:/in/pt:ro
       ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/pt:/in/pt:z if you are using shared volume/storage
       The syntax for the volumes definition is <directory_on_host>:<directory_in_container>:<options>. The
       first line means that the directory CONTAINER_HOME/in/pt on the Linux host will map to in/pt in the
       container, and the volume will be read-only (ro). The second line uses the option z for shared volume or
       storage.
    •  <[image_name:tag]>
       The name for the container image; for example, peopletools:8.6x.
    •  --network
       (Optional): ps-net
       Note. If you plan to use a search component (OSK) DPK with a mid-tier container on a Podman network
       on same host, create the Podman network before running the mid-tier container. Use this command:
       podman network create --driver bridge ps-net
3.  To remove a container, run the following commands to stop, remove, and start:
    podman stop <container-name> -t<time in secs>
    podman rm <container-name>
    podman start <container-name>
    The podman stop command will shut down all PeopleTools domains gracefully before the container stops.
    View container stop logs simultaneously using the command podman logs -fn <container-name>
    in a new terminal window. Add the option -t<time in secs> to the command to specify the time in
    seconds to gracefully shut down all PeopleTools domains followed by the database instance before the
    container exits.
    The podman start command will start all PeopleTools domains. View container start logs simultaneously
    by using the command podman logs -fn <container-name> in a new terminal window.
278                                                                         Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix E                                                                        Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers
Option 2: Specify the parameters for running the container image in the compose.yaml file, and then run a
podman-compose command:
1.  Open CONTAINER_HOME/compose.yaml for editing.
2.  Enter the container image name for the image parameter, in the format image_name:tag. In this example, the
    image name is oracle/peopletools, and the tag is 8.6x.
    Refer to the previous section for descriptions of the remaining parameters.
    version: 1
    services:
      mt:
           image: oracle/peopletools:8.6x
           hostname: mt.oraclevcn.com
           container_name: mt.oraclevcn.com
           network: net
           tty: true
           ports:
           - "${PORT_RMT_ADMIN:-10100}:10100"
           - "${PORT_RMT_ADMIN_RMI:-10101}:10101"
           - "${PORT_WSL:-7000-7003}:7000-7003"
           - "${PORT_WSL_SSL:-7010}:7010"
           - "${PORT_PIA:-8000}:8000"
           - "${PORT_PIA_SSL:-8001}:8001"
           environment:
           - DB_WAIT_TIMEOUT=1
           volumes:
           - ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/pt:/in/pt:ro
    networks:
      net:
           name: ps-net
           driver: bridge
3.  Run this command:
    podman-compose -f ${CONTAINER_HOME}/compose.yaml up -d
4.  To view the container logs, open a new terminal window and enter this command.
    podman logs <container-name>
    The container is up and running when it shows the following message in the podman logs:
    ============================
    PEOPLETOOLS IS READY TO USE!
    ============================
5.  Remove CONTAINER_HOME/in/pt/install.rsp once the container is up and running. The install.rsp is not
    stored anywhere in the container.
    rm ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/pt/install.rsp
6.  To log in to the container as the psadm2 user, which is created by the DPK setup script, enter this command:
    podman exec -ti <container-name> su - psadm2
7.  To access the PIA, use http://<Container Host FQDN>:8000.
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                           279
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                                  Appendix E
    Enter this command to return the PIA URL:
    echo "http://$(hostname -f):${PORT_PIA:-8000}"
8.  To view the DPK logs, log in to <container-name>.
    If the deployment was successful, the logs will be in /ps/psft_dpk_setup.log.
    If the deployment failed, the logs will be in /in/tmp/setup/psft_dpk_setup.log.
    Use these commands to view and copy the DPK logs to the current location on the Linux VM:
    podman exec -ti <container-name> vi /in/tmp/setup/psft_dpk_setup.log
    podman cp -a <container-name>:/in/tmp/setup/psft_dpk_setup.log
In case the deployment results in errors, you can find information on error messages associated with the
PeopleSoft containers in CONTAINER_HOME/FAQ.md.
Task E-2-5: Starting and Stopping the PeopleTools Mid-tier Container
Use this command to stop the container. This command gracefully shuts down all PeopleTools domains and then
exits the container.
podman stop <container-name> -t<time in secs>
To view logs for the shutdown simultaneously, open a new terminal and enter this command:
podman logs -fn <container-name>
Use this command to start the container. This command starts all PeopleTools domains.
podman start <container-name>
Use this command to start the container and view the logs:
podman start <container-name> ; podman logs -fnl
To view logs simultaneously, open a new terminal and enter this command.
podman logs -fn <container-name>
Task E-2-6: Removing the PeopleTools Mid-tier Container
Use this command to stop and remove the container. This command gracefully shuts down all PeopleTools
domains and then exits the container. Specify the number of seconds to allow for the domains to shut down in
<time in secs>.
podman-compose -f ${CONTAINER_NAME}/compose.yaml down -t<time in secs>
Alternatively, use these commands to stop and remove the container.
podman-compose stop <container-name> -t<time in secs>
podman-compose rm <container-name>
To view logs simultaneously, open a new terminal and enter this command.
podman-compose -f ${CONTAINER_NAME}/compose.yaml down -t<time in secs>

Task E-3-2: Preparing to Deploy the OSK Container
To prepare to deploy the OSK (OpenSearch, OpenSearch Dashboards, and Logstash) container:
1.  Review the instructions in CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/osk/README.md.
2.  Copy the OSK DPK zip files into CONTAINER_HOME/in/osk.
    Don't unzip the files. Don't use symbolic links.
3.  Review the installation command in CONTAINER_HOME/in/osk/install-osk.file, but do not make any
    changes.
    The command deploys the OSK DPK in silent installation mode.
4.  Open CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/osk/pt_osk.config for editing.
    The pt-osk.config file is the silent installation response file for OpenSearch. This file is called by the silent
    installation command, which is contained in install-osk.file in the same ./in/osk directory. Use the response
    file sample in the Search Components installation guide and modify it for your requirements.
    See PeopleSoft Deployment Package Installation for Search Components, "Installing OpenSearch and
    OpenSearch Dashboards on Linux in Silent Mode."
    Verify these values in pt-osk.config:
    •  For IB_REST_URL, enter the Integration Broker REST service URL in the PeopleSoft mid-tier container
       deployment.
       To locate the REST service URL, see the Integration Broker product documentation.
       See PeopleTools: Integration Broker Administration, "Using the Target Locations Page to Set Target
       Locations for Services."
    •  Enter encrypted user ID and passwords, as described in .the Search Components installation guide.
       See PeopleSoft Deployment Package Installation for Search Components, "Installing OpenSearch and
       OpenSearch Dashboards on Linux in Silent Mode."

    name.

Task E-3-4: Running the OSK Container Image
This section describes two options to run the container image. Use the podman command and define the setup
with command parameters, or edit a compose-osk.yaml file and run a podman-compose command.
For both options, first ensure that the CONTAINER_HOME environment variable is set to the location of the
OraclePeopleTools directory that holds the container files in your environment.
export CONTAINER_HOME=<Path to Container Code directory OraclePeopleTools>
Option 1: To run the OSK container image with the podman command:
1.  Ensure that the PeopleTools mid-tier container is up and running.
    Sign in to PIA for the PeopleTools mid-tier container at least once before running the OSK container.
2.  Set these environment variables with the values for the PeopleTools mid-tier container image:
    export MIDTIER_SERVICE_HOST=<MIDTIER HOST FQDN / IP>
    export MIDTIER_SERVICE_PORT_HTTP=<MIDTIER PORT HTTP>
    export IB_NODE_NAME=<IB NODE NAME> # example PSFT_LOCAL
    export DBNAME=<DB Name>
3.  Change to the CONTAINER_HOME/in/osk directory.
    cd ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/osk
4.  Run this command:
    podman run -d --name <osk container name> \
    --hostname <container hostname> \
    -e <env. variables> \
    -p <Add Container Port Mapping> \
    -v [<host mount point>:]/in/pt \
    <[image_name:tag]>
    Use these guidelines for the command parameters:
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                            283
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                                   Appendix E
   •  --name
      (Optional): The name of the container. If no name is entered, it will be auto generated by default. For
      example: osk.oraclevcn.com
   •  --hostname
      The parameter <osk container hostname> will be used as the host name for the container. PeopleTools
      uses the hostname for various processes, so it is important to set the hostname properly.
      The container hostname must not contain any underscores ( _ ).
      Do not use the FQDN of the VM host or machine where the container runs for <container hostname>. For
      example, if your mid-tier container VM host or machine FQDN is server.example.one.oraclevcn.com, then
      set <osk container hostname> as osk.oraclevcn.com.
      This will set:
      hostname -f to osk.oraclevcn.com — container long host name (FQDN)
      hostname -d to oraclevcn.com — container DNS domain name
      hostname -a to osk— container alias name
284                                                                         Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.

   •  -e
      Specify the environment variables listed in this table. To specify multiple environment variables, preface
      each one with -e. For example:
      -e "OPENSEARCH_JAVA_OPTS=-Xms512m -Xmx512m" \
      -e bootstrap.memory_lock=true \
      -e IB_REST_URL="http://${MIDTIER_SERVICE_HOST}:${MIDTIER_SERVICE_PORT_⇒
      HTTP}/PSIGW/RESTListeningConnector/${IB_NODE_NAME}/PT_CREATEJSON_⇒
      REST.v1/json="
               Environment Variable                      Comment
              MT_WAIT_TIMEOUT=<wait time for MT in       This environment variable is optional. Enter the time
              seconds>                                   to wait for the connection to the mid-tier container.
              "OPENSEARCH_JAVA_OPTS=-Xms512m -           This is required for the OSK deployment.
              Xmx512m"
              bootstrap.memory_lock=true                 Set to true to disable JVM heap memory swapping.
              IB_REST_URL="http://${MIDTIER_SERVICE_H    For IB_REST_URL, enter the Integration Broker
              OST}:${MIDTIER_SERVICE_PORT_HTTP}/PSI      REST service URL in the PeopleSoft mid-tier

Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                         285
Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers                                                                  Appendix E
    •  –p
       The port mapping of the host port to the container port, in the format < host ports >:< fixed container ports
       >.
       Ensure that the ports are not in use. Each container must use different ports.
              Container Type                                 Port Mapping String
             osk                                             -p 9200:9200 -p 9300:9300 -p 9800:9800 -p
                                                             5601:5601
    •  -v
       Enter one of the following for the host mount point:
       ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/osk:/in/osk:ro
       ${CONTAINER_HOME}/in/osk:/in/osk:z if you are using shared volume/storage
       The syntax for the volumes definition is <directory_on_host>:<directory_in_container>:<options>. The
       first line means that the directory CONTAINER_HOME/in/pt on the Linux host will map to in/pt in the
       container, and the volume will be read-only (ro). The second line uses the option z for shared volume or
       storage.
    •  <[image_name:tag]>
       The name for the container image; for example, peopletools:8.6x.
    •  --network
       (Optional): ps-net
       Using the same value for this variable ensures the mid-tier and OSK containers are running on the same
       Podman network.
5.  Verify that the container is up and running.
    The container is up and running when it shows the following message in the container logs:
    ================================
    PEOPLETOOLS OSK IS READY TO USE!
    ================================

Task E-3-6: Integrating PeopleTools Mid-tier with OpenSearch
This is an optional task. After you build containers for PeopleTools mid-tier and search components, use these
steps to integrate OpenSearch and the PeopleSoft environment using Automated Configuration Management
(ACM) plug-ins.
To set up the PTSFConfigureSrchInstance plug-in:
1.  Create a property file named OSK_SEARCH_TEMPLATE.
    Use the sample given in CONTAINER_HOME/containerfiles/osk/README.md as a guide, and modify it for
    your environment.
    Note. The sample includes parameters with "Kibana." Be aware that Kibana is not supported for PeopleTools
    8.62. These parameters are used for configuration for OpenSearch Dashboards. The sample parameters are
    subject to change in future releases of the OSK DPK. Review the product documentation when preparing your
    template.
    tee -a "OSK_SEARCH_TEMPLATE" <<EOF
    configure=true
    verify=true
    decrypt_password=false
    #TEMPLATE VARIABLES SECTION
    template.domain=@webhist.SESSIONCOOKIEDOM
    template.host=@webhist.WEBSERVERNAME
    template.httpport=@webhist.PORTALHTTPPORT
    template.nodename=%LocalNode
    template.searchhost=search.com
    template.searchport=9200
    template.sslport=@webhist.PORTALHTTPSPORT
    template.userid=%UserId
    #END: TEMPLATE VARIABLES SECTION
    ########################################
    #Configure Peoplesoft system to talk to Search Server and assign roles
    ########################################
    plugin.0=PTEM_CONFIG:PTSFConfigureSrchInstance
    env.search_nodes=1
    env.search_provider=OS

Task E-4: Working With PeopleSoft Containers
After the container is up and running, you can connect to the PeopleSoft environment in the container from a
Microsoft Windows-based PeopleTools Client to use utilities such as Change Assistant. Use the IP address for the
Linux VM, the port numbers from the setup YAML file, and the user IDs and passwords from the silent response
file for the connection.
Use these commands to review and maintain the containers. These are examples of commands to perform actions
without logging into the container.
•  To perform a health check on the container:
   podman ps -a --filter "name=<container-name>"
   podman healthcheck run <container-name>
•  To list all of the port mappings in use in the container:
   podman port -a
•  To display the Podman disk usage:
   podman system df -v
•  To inspect container or image information:
   podman inspect <name>
•  To log in to the container as the psadm2 user, which is one of the default Linux users that is created by the
   DPK setup script.
   podman exec -ti <container-name> su - psadm2
•  To view the PeopleSoft PIA URL:
   echo "http://$(hostname -f):${PORT_PIA:-8000}"

•  To view the peopletools.properties file:
   podman exec -ti <container-name> su - psadm2 -c 'cat ${PS_HOME}⇒
   /peopletools.properties'
•  To view the PeopleTools release:
   podman exec -ti <container-name> su - psadm2 -c 'psreleaseinfo'
•  To view the location of PS_HOME:

Understanding the Oracle Kubernetes Engine Deployment
 Run PeopleSoft containers Oracle Kubernetes Engine (OKE) on Oracle Cloud Infrastructure. OKE uses
 Kubernetes, which is an open-source system for automating deployment, scaling, and management of
 containerized applications across clusters of hosts. This process supports the deployment of PeopleTools mid-tier
 and search components (OpenSearch, OpenSearch Dashboards, Logstash).
 See Overview of Kubernetes Engine (OKE) in Oracle Cloud Infrastructure documentation,
 https://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/ContEng/Concepts/contengoverview.htm.
 Read these sections if you want to deploy PeopleSoft containers on a Kubernetes cluster (OKE):
 •  Understanding the Oracle Kubernetes Engine Deployment
 •  Prerequisites for the OKE Deployment
 •  Building Container Images and Pushing to Oracle Container Registry
 •  Creating File System Storage and Uploading DPKs
 •  Creating and Accessing the Kubernetes Cluster
 •  Creating and Configuring an Ingress NGINX Load Balancer
 •  Creating Oracle Container Registry Secret
 •  Running PeopleSoft Containers on the Kubernetes Cluster
 You also have the option to deploy PeopleSoft containers on a Kubernetes cluster (OKE) with Helm. Helm is a
 package manager for Kubernetes that helps to manage deployment, scaling, and upgrades.
 Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                        293
Deploying PeopleSoft Containers on Oracle Kubernetes Engine                                          Appendix F
See Using Helm, https://helm.sh/docs/intro/using_helm.
Read these sections if you want to deploy PeopleSoft containers on a Kubernetes cluster with Helm:
•  Understanding the Oracle Kubernetes Engine Deployment
•  Prerequisites for the OKE Deployment
•  Building Container Images and Pushing to Oracle Container Registry
•  Creating File System Storage and Uploading DPKs
•  Creating and Accessing the Kubernetes Cluster
•  Creating and Configuring an Ingress NGINX Load Balancer
•  Creating Oracle Container Registry Secret
•  Running PeopleSoft Containers on the Kubernetes Cluster with Helm
See Also
Kubernetes Evolution: Streamlining Deployments with Helm,
https://blogs.oracle.com/developers/post/kubernetes-evolution-streamlining-deployments-with-helm
Prerequisites for the OKE Deployment
Before beginning the deployment, you must fulfill the following requirements:
•  Access to My Oracle Support to download the DPKs
   Download the four DPKs for PeopleTools mid-tier, and the search components DPK (OpenSearch,
   OpenSearch Dashboads, and Logstash, or OSK DPK). You will use the DPKs to install PS_HOME, to create
   container images, and to deploy the container images to OKE.
•  Oracle Cloud Infrastructure account
   Verify that the Oracle Cloud Account includes subscriptions for Compute, Block Storage, File Storage
   service, and Oracle Kubernetes Engine (OKE). You will also need a subscription to Database Cloud Service if
   you host your database on Oracle Cloud Infrastructure.
•  Knowledge of OKE administration, Kubernetes clusters, OKE and Kubernetes commands.
•  Access to Oracle Container Registry
   The Oracle Linux 8 platform image for the installation will be downloaded from this location during the
   container build process. After you create the container image, you upload it to the registry to be used by OKE.
   See Oracle Container Registry, https://container-registry.oracle.com.
•  An Oracle Linux VM
   The Oracle Linux VM is the host for the container, and must be configured to use Podman as described in this
   documentation.
•  Podman and podman-compose
   Each user that builds and runs containers and the prebuilt container images must be able to run Podman and
   podman-compose. The task Configuring the Linux VM for Container Deployment includes steps to verify that
   Podman and podman-compose are available.
•  A non-root user with sudo root permission
   To configure the Linux VM, build and run containers, the user must be able to use the sudo command.
•  Access to Helm and familiarity to Helm commands.
294                                                                          Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix F                                                      Deploying PeopleSoft Containers on Oracle Kubernetes Engine
Task F-1: Building Container Images and Pushing to Oracle
Container Registry
To build the container images:
1.  Create a virtual machine (VM) instance in OCI Compute with an Oracle Linux 8 operating system.
    See Creating an Instance in the OCI documentation, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
    us/iaas/Content/Compute/Tasks/launchinginstance.htm.
2.  In OCI, provision a block volume and attach it to the instance.
    See Working with Block Volumes in the OCI documentation, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
    us/iaas/Content/Block/blockvolumes.htm.
3.  Install and configure Podman on the instance.
    See "Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers," Configuring the Linux VM for Container Deployment.
4.  Build the container images on the Compute instance.
    See "Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers," Building and Running the Container for Mid-tier
    Deployment.
    See "Deploying PeopleSoft DPKs in Containers," Building and Running the Container for Search
    Components.
5.  Push the container image to Oracle Cloud Infrastructure Registry, which the OCI documentation refers to as
    Container Registry.
    See Overview of Container Registry, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
    us/iaas/Content/Registry/Concepts/registryoverview.htm.
    See Pushing Images Using the Docker CLI, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
    us/iaas/Content/Registry/Tasks/registrypushingimagesusingthedockercli.htm.
Task F-2: Creating File System Storage and Uploading DPKs
OCI File System storage is used to store the PeopleTools DPKs. The DPKs are required after building the
container images for deployment in OKE.
1.  Create a File Storage service file system and mount point.
    See Create File Systems in the Oracle Cloud Infrastructure documentation, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
    us/iaas/Content/File/Tasks/creatingfilesystems.htm.
2.  Obtain the PeopleTools and search DPKs from My Oracle Support.
3.  Upload the DPKs to the OCI file system.
    See Transferring Data To and From File Storage in the Oracle Cloud Infrastructure documentation,
    https://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/File/Tasks/transferring-data-to-and-from-file-storage.htm.
Task F-3: Creating and Accessing the Kubernetes Cluster
To set up the Kubernetes cluster:
1.  Create a Kubernetes cluster in OCI.
Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.                                                            295
 Deploying PeopleSoft Containers on Oracle Kubernetes Engine                                             Appendix F
     The Quick Create feature creates a cluster with default settings and a new Virtual Cloud Network (VCN).
     The Kubernetes cluster node pool is a cluster of Compute instances. Specify enough nodes to meet your
     requirements. For example, create a cluster with four nodes for four PeopleTools Compute instances.
     See Using the Console to create a Cluster with Default Settings in the 'Quick Create' workflow,
     https://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/ContEng/Tasks/contengcreatingclusterusingoke_topic-
     Using_the_Console_to_create_a_Quick_Cluster_with_Default_Settings.htm.
 2.  Create a Compute instance with a Linux 8 operating system to access and work with the Kubernetes cluster.
     The cluster access instance must be in the same VCN and same subnet as the Kubernetes cluster.
     See Creating an Instance in the Oracle Cloud Infrastructure documentation,
     https://docs.oracle.com/enus/iaas/Content/Compute/Tasks/launchinginstance.htm.
 3.  Configure the instance to access the Kubernetes cluster.
     See Setting Up Cluster Access in the Oracle Cloud Infrastructure documentation, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
     us/iaas/Content/ContEng/Tasks/contengdownloadkubeconfigfile.htm.
 4.  Install kubectl on the Kubernetes cluster control plane.
     For information on the kubectl commands cited in this documentation, see the Kubernetes documentation.
     You can also use the command kubectl -h to see help on the command line.
     See Kubernetes documentation, https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl.
 5.  Log in to each node in the cluster and extend the root partition.
     The node partitions do not expand automatically, so it is necessary to extend them after creating the OKE
     cluster.
     See Extending the Root Partition of Worker Nodes, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
     us/iaas/Content/ContEng/Tasks/contengextendingrootpartitionmanually.htm.
Task F-4: Creating and Configuring an Ingress NGINX Load
Balancer
This section discusses:
 •   Installing Ingress NGINX Controller
 •   Configuring NGINX Deployment
 •   Configuring NGINX Service
 Task F-4-1: Installing Ingress NGINX Controller
 Set up an ingress NGINX controller to load balance and handle HTTP and HTTPS traffic originating from outside
 a Kubernetes cluster. The NGINX controller is a third-party ingress controller that you install and configure
 separately from the Kubernetes cluster. Use the latest version of the NGINX controller.
 See Managing Ingress Controllers, in the Oracle Cloud Infrastructure documentation, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
 us/iaas/Content/ContEng/Tasks/contengmanagingresscontrollers.htm.
 To install the controller:
 1.  Locate the version number for the latest version of the controller in the GitHub documentation.
     You can find the versions listed in Supported Versions table in the Readme section. For example, the latest
     version at the time of this publication is 1.12.0.
 296                                                                       Copyright © 2025, Oracle and/or its affiliates.
Appendix F                                                     Deploying PeopleSoft Containers on Oracle Kubernetes Engine
    See GitHub, kubernetes/ingress-nginx, https://github.com/kubernetes/ingress-nginx.
2.  Enter this kubectl command to create the load balancer, substituting the version number for <vnum>:
    kubectl apply -f https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/ingress-⇒
    nginx/controller-v<vnum>/deploy/static/provider/cloud/deploy.yaml
    See Deploying the Ingress Controller and associated resources, in the Oracle Cloud Infrastructure
    Documentation, https://docs.oracle.com/en-
    us/iaas/Content/ContEng/Tasks/contengsettingupingresscontroller.htm#settingupcontroller__creatingserviceac
    count.
